\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in Q^{\prime}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
, then
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{Iterate}(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}})
is nonempty and for every
a_{v}
emitted by
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{Iterate}(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}})
, we have
(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},a_{v})\in Q(\mathcal{D})
.

Proof.

Consider two items.

(1)
Assume that
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma}\in Q(\mathcal{D})
.
Let
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma}
.
Hence
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma}
satisfies all positive atoms of
Q
, and therefore
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
must satisfy all positive atoms of
Q^{\prime}
by the
\alpha
-step.

Suppose for contradiction that
\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in R_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}
for some
i\in[m]
.
Therefore, we have that
\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}R_{U}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{nextM}(\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}})
is empty.
This means that for every
a_{v}
such that

(\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},a_{v})\in R_{U}
in
\mathcal{D}
, there is some
j\in[i]
such that
(\Pi_{N_{j}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},a_{v})\in R_{N_{j}}
. Then in particular, there is some
j\in[i]
such that
(\Pi_{N_{j}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},\Pi_{\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma})=\Pi_{N_{j}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma}\in R_{N_{j}}
.
However, since
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma}\in Q(\mathcal{D})
,
\Pi_{N_{j}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma}\notin R_{N_{j}}
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\notin\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}R_{N_{i}}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
for every
i\in[m]
, and
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in Q^{\prime}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
, as desired.

(2)
Assume that
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in Q^{\prime}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.
Then, we have
\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in R_{U\setminus\{v\}}
, and thus
\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{L}_{v}
.
First, we argue that
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{Iterate}(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}})
is nonempty.
Indeed, if
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{Iterate}(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}})
is empty, then
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{nextM}(\mathbf{a}_{N_{m}})
must also be empty since
N_{m}
is the largest in the chain
U\subseteq N_{1}\subseteq N_{2}\subseteq\dots\subseteq N_{m}
.
Therefore, there exists some
i\in[m]
such that
\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}R_{N_{i}}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.
However, since
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in Q^{\prime}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
,
\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\notin\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}R_{N_{i}}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, a contradiction.

Let
a_{v}
be an arbitrary constant iterated by
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{Iterate}(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}})
.
We show that
(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},a_{v})\in Q(\mathcal{D})
.
Since
\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in Q^{\prime}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
,
\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}R_{U}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, and by construction,

(\Pi_{U\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},a_{v})\in R_{U}
in
\mathcal{D}
and thus satisfies every positive atom in
Q
by the
\alpha
-step.
It remains to show that
(\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},a_{v})\notin R_{N_{i}}
for every
i\in[m]
.
Indeed, if
(\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}},a_{v})\in R_{N_{i}}
for some
i\in[m]
, then
a_{v}
would have been skipped by a skipping link labeled
\Pi_{N_{i}\setminus\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
, and thus will not be iterated by
\mathcal{L}_{v}.\mathsf{Iterate}(\mathbf{a}_{\sigma^{\prime}})
, a contradiction.

The proof is now complete.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

We first prove the theorem for full queries.

Given a signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
, its signed-elimination sequence can be found in time
O(|Q|^{3})
using brute force:
we may find a signed leaf in
\mathcal{H}
in time
O(|Q|^{2})
by first iterating over every vertex and then checking whether it is a signed leaf by definition. Then we remove this signed leaf from
\mathcal{H}
, and iteratively apply the previous process until the graph is empty within
O(|Q|)
iterations. We remark that this step can potentially be improved.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be the hypergraph of
Q
before the preprocessing step. For every vertex
v
in
\mathcal{H}
, we denote
d(v)
as the number of positive and negative hyperedges in
\mathcal{H}
that contains
v
. A key observation is that: for any data structure
\mathcal{L}_{v}
, the procedures
\mathsf{prevM}
,
\mathsf{nextM}
runs in time
O(d(v))
, since there are at most
d(v)
projections to check.
For implementation, we also need to add create a book keeping hash table from each tuple
\mathbf{a}_{U}
to the exact node in the hash table
\mathcal{L}_{v}
that contains the value
\Pi_{\{v\}}\mathbf{a}_{U}
for later use.
Therefore, the
\alpha
-step runs in
O(d(v)\cdot|R_{U}|)
time, since for each tuple in
R_{U}
, we need to probe at most
d(v)
relations to process it. For the
\beta
-step, the data structure
\mathcal{L}_{v}
can be initialized in
O(|R_{U}|)
time for line
11
.
Note that line
4
of
\mathsf{ExtendList}(\mathcal{L}_{v},N_{i})
only takes constant time using the book keeping hash table created in the
\alpha
-step.
For line
15
–
16
and each
i\in\{1,2,\dots,m\}
, the running time is
O(d(v)\cdot|R_{N_{i}}|)
, since for each tuple in
R_{N_{i}}
,
\mathsf{nextM}
and
\mathsf{prevM}
are called, both requiring
O(d(v))
time.

Hence one recursive step of the preprocessing phase runs in
O(d(v)\cdot(|R_{U}|+|R_{N_{1}}|+|R_{N_{2}}|+\dots|R_{N_{m}}|))=O(d(v)\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
time.
Summing over all possible vertex
v
, we have that the preprocessing phase runs in time
\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}O(d(v)\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)=O(|Q|\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
.

For the enumeration phase, let
(c_{1},c_{2},\dots,c_{n})
and
(c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},\dots,c_{n}^{\prime})
be two consecutive answers enumerated by Algorithm
4
.
Let
i
be the largest such that
c_{j}=c_{j}^{\prime}
for every
1\leq j\leq i
.
For every
i+1\leq j\leq n
,
\mathcal{L}_{v_{j}}.\mathsf{nextM}((c_{1},c_{2},\dots,c_{j}))
is called and returns
\bot
, incurring a running time of
O(d({v_{j}}))
.
Hence the overall delay is
\sum_{i+1\leq j\leq n}O(d({v_{j}}))=O(|Q|)
.

Next, we consider the case where
Q
is free-connex signed-acyclic with signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
and free variables
F=[f]
for an integer
0\leq f<n
.
Then the signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\{F\})
is signed-acyclic.
By Proposition
3.5
, there exists a signed-elimination sequence

\sigma=(1,2,\dots,f)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}.

To enumerate
Q(\mathcal{D})
, the crux is to apply Algorithm
1
on inputs
(\mathcal{H}^{\prime},\mathcal{D},\sigma)
in the preprocessing phase,
as if

Q
were a full query.
Subsequently in the enumeration step, we only uses the list data structures
\mathcal{L}_{1}
,
\mathcal{L}_{2}
,
\dots
,
\mathcal{L}_{f}
, ignoring every
\mathcal{L}_{v}
where
v
appears in the sequence
\sigma^{\prime}
.
This is correct, because every answer to
Q(\mathcal{D})
must also participate in the first
f
positions of some full query answers, which can be recovered exactly by traversing only the first
f
list data structures.

∎

Appendix C
Missing Details for Section
5
: Enumeration Algorithms for
\mathsf{FAQ}^{\neg}
and
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}

Context-free Grammar (
CFG
)

If
u,v,w
are strings of terminals and non-terminals, and
\ell::=w
is a rule of the
CFG
, we say that
u\ell v

yields

uwv
in the
CFG
(written as
u\ell v\Rightarrow uwv
). We say that
u

derives

v
(written as
u\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}v
) in the
CFG
if
u=v
or if there is a sequence
u_{1},\dots,u_{k}
for
k\geq 0
such that

u\Rightarrow u_{1}\Rightarrow u_{2}\Rightarrow\cdots\Rightarrow u_{k}\Rightarrow v.

A
derivation
of a string for a grammar is a sequence of grammar rule applications that transform the start symbol into the string. A derivation proves that the string belongs to the grammar’s language.

\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
Grammar & Expression

We formally introduce
nested functional aggregate queries with negation
(
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
) over a single semiring
\mathbf{S}=(\boldsymbol{D},\oplus,\otimes,\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1})
. As motivated in Section
5
,
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
is a generalization of
\mathsf{FAQ}^{\neg}
that is essential for our enumeration algorithms.

For any
i\in[n]
, let
x_{i}
denote a variable, and
a_{i}
denote a value in the discrete domain
\mathsf{Dom}(x_{i})
of the variable
x_{i}
.
Overloading notation, we also refer to
[n]
as the set of variables.
For any subset
K\subseteq[n]
, we define
\mathbf{x}_{K}=(x_{i})_{i\in K}
and
\mathbf{a}_{K}=(a_{i})_{i\in K}\in\prod_{i\in K}\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{K})
, where
\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{K})=\prod_{i\in K}\mathsf{Dom}(x_{i})
. In other words,
\mathbf{x}_{K}
is a tuple of variables, and
\mathbf{a}_{K}
is a tuple of specific values with support
K
.

Let
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
be a signed hypergraph. To each
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
(and
K\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
), we associate a distinct function
R_{K}:\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\rightarrow\boldsymbol{D}
, called a
factor
. We assume that all factors are represented via the
listing representation
: each factor
R_{K}
is a table of all tuples of the form
\langle\mathbf{a}_{K},R_{K}(\mathbf{a}_{K})\rangle
, where
R_{K}(\mathbf{a}_{K})\in\boldsymbol{D}
is the
weight
of the tuple
\mathbf{a}_{K}
. For entries not in the table, the factor implicitly encodes their weights as
\mathbf{0}
. Under set-theoretic context, we also use
R_{K}
to denote the set of tuples of schema
\mathbf{x}_{K}
explicitly stored in the factor table and
\neg R_{K}=\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\setminus R_{K}
. Our definition of listing representation is slightly more general than
(
Khamis et al. 2016
;
Khamis et al. 2020
)
in that we allow
R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})=\mathbf{0}
for some
\mathbf{x}_{K}
in the table (i.e.
\mathbf{x}_{K}\in R_{K}
). A factor
R_{K}
where
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
(resp.
R_{N}
where
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
) is called a
positive
(resp.
negative
)
factor
.

Over a single semiring
\mathbf{S}=(\boldsymbol{D},\oplus,\otimes,\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1})
, a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
associated with
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
is a string recognized by the following context-free grammar (
CFG
):

(5)

\displaystyle\framebox{{CFG}}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }S\subseteq[n],

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle::=\;e

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=\emptyset\text{ and }e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle::=\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=K\cup S^{-},K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}\text{ and }S^{-}\subseteq S

\displaystyle\mid\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\cup S^{-},N^{-}\subseteq N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\text{ and }S^{-}\subseteq S

where

•

(terminals)
e,R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})
and
R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})
, where
e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\},K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
and
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
, the semiring operators
\otimes
and
\vdash
, and the parenthesis are terminals of the
CFG
. On its semantics,
R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})
is a shorthand for
R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\oplus(\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N}}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))
, where
N^{-}\subseteq N
and

\mathbbm{1}_{{R_{N}}}
is an indicator factor defined as the following (also defined in
(
Khamis et al. 2020
)
):

\mathbbm{1}_{{R_{N}}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})=\begin{cases}\mathbf{1}&\text{ if }\mathbf{x}_{K}\in R_{K}\\
\mathbf{0}&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

Here,
\vdash
is the non-commutative operator that takes a negative factor as its left operand and a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression as its right operand.

•

(non-terminals)
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
, where
S\subseteq[n]
, are the non-terminals of the
CFG
, and in particular (
S=[n]
),
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
is the start symbol of the
CFG
. A
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression is a string
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
derived from the
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
non-terminal, following a sequence of grammar rule applications of
CFG
as defined in (
5
).

•

(size measure) the
size
of a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
is defined as
|\psi|=\sum_{i\in[n]}d(i)
, where
d(i)
is the number of hyperedges in
\mathcal{H}
that contain
i
;

•

(our assumptions) in our work, we assume that each factor
R_{K},R_{N}
, where
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+},N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
, shows up precisely once in
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
, i.e., a one-to-one mapping from the hyperedges of
\mathcal{H}
to factors in
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
. Moreover, we assume that a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
is
safe
, that is, every
i\in[n]
appears in some
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
(i.e.
\bigcup_{{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}}}K=[n]
). Indeed, the “unsafest” case occurs when every branch takes the right child of
\vdash
operator (thus more propensity to have un-guarded variables), because if takes the left child
R_{N}
, then the right sub-tree is immediately guarded by
R_{N}
; otherwise, the
\neg R_{N}
can possibly be un-guarded.

A
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
can be depicted pictorially as a
abstract syntax tree
(
\mathsf{AST}
) drawn below, where

\bigotimes
\vdash
\vdash
\vdash
e
R_{K_{1}}
R_{K_{t}}
\cdots
\cdots
R_{N_{k}}
\bigotimes
R_{N_{k-1}}
\bigotimes
R_{N_{1}}
\bigotimes
\cdots
\cdots
\cdots

Figure 5.
The
\mathsf{AST}
for a general
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
recognized by the
CFG
in (
5
).

•

\bigotimes
and
\vdash
are the intermediate nodes, and they are show up on alternating levels, starting from the root being a
\bigotimes
node. The
\bigotimes
node takes at least one child, but the
\vdash
node always takes two children.

•

e,R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})
and
R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})
, where
e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\},K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
and
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
, are the leaves of the
\mathsf{AST}
.

The
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
Queries.

A
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query
\varphi
(with free variables
F\subseteq[n]
) associated with a signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
is defined as:

(6)

\displaystyle\varphi(\mathbf{x}_{F})=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{x}_{[n]\setminus F}\in\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]\setminus F})}\;\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})

where

•

\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
is a safe
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression recognized by the
CFG
in (
5
). Sometimes we call
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
a
full

\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query (i.e.
F=[n]
).

•

F=[f]\subseteq[n]
is the set of
free variables
for some integer
0\leq f\leq n
.

•

(query input and output) The input to a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query
\varphi
is a database instance
\mathcal{D}
that contains the constants
e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
and a list representation for every factor in
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
. The size of a database instance
|\mathcal{D}|
is defined as the total number of rows to store the constants and the list representations. An
answer
(or
output
) of
\varphi
is a tuple
\langle\mathbf{a}_{F},\varphi(\mathbf{a}_{F})\rangle\in\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{F})
such that
\mathbf{a}_{F}\in\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{F})
and
\varphi(\mathbf{a}_{F})\neq\mathbf{0}
. The set of all answers of
\varphi
is denoted by
\varphi(\mathcal{D})
.

•

(complexity measure) In this work, we measure the combined (and data) complexity of evaluating a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query
\varphi
on a database instance
\mathcal{D}
by the query size
|\varphi|=|\psi|=\sum_{i\in[n]}d(i)
and the database size
|\mathcal{D}|
.

A fragement of
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
queries is the
\mathsf{FAQ}^{\neg}
queries, where the corresponding
\mathsf{AST}
is flat: the right child of the
\vdash
nodes are
\mathbf{c}_{N}\neq\mathbf{0}
, that is, the default values of
R_{N}
. Indeed, the query can be compactly written as

\displaystyle\varphi(\mathbf{x}_{F})=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{x}_{[n]\setminus F}\in\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]\setminus F})}\;\bigotimes_{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}}R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\bigotimes_{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mathbf{c}_{N})

that coincides with the definition of
\mathsf{FAQ}^{\neg}
queries in (
3
) by letting
\overline{R}_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})=(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mathbf{c}_{N})
.

Definition C.1 (Free-connex
signed
-acyclicity).

A
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query
\varphi
(
6
) is
free-connex
signed
-acyclic
if the signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-}\cup\{F\})
is
signed
-acyclic.

In the following sections, we will show the following main theorem for free-connex
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
queries, which subsumes Theorem
1.2
as a special case.

Theorem C.2.

Let
\varphi
be a free-connex
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query with free variables
F
over a commutative semiring
\mathbf{S}
. Then, there is an algorithm that can enumerate the answers of
\varphi
in
O(|\varphi|)
delay, after a
O(|\varphi|^{3}+|\varphi|\cdot|\mathcal{D}|\cdot\alpha(14\cdot|\mathcal{D}|,|\mathcal{D}|))
preprocessing time.

Before jumping to the general case of free-connex
signed
-acyclicity, we dedicate the next section to a special case, where
\varphi
is a
full

signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query, i.e.
F=[n]
.

C.1.
Enumeration of full
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}

In this section, we study the enumeration problem
\mathsf{Enum}(\varphi,\mathcal{D})
for
signed
-acyclic
full

\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
queries. First, we recall the definition (
6
) and (
5
) that: a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query associated with a signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
is full if
F=[n]
, that is,
\varphi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
, where
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
is a safe
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression associated with
\mathcal{H}
. The enumeration algorithm can be summarized as follows.

(1)

(pre-processing phase) we first reduce the
\mathsf{Enum}(\varphi,\mathcal{D})
problem into a
\mathsf{Enum}(Q^{*},\mathcal{D}^{*})
problem, where
Q^{*}
is a
signed
-acyclic
full

\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
query that evaluates on a new database instance
\mathcal{D}^{*}
; then, we follow the pre-processing algorithm for
signed
-acyclic
full

\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
queries presented in Section
4
.

(2)

(enumeration phase) we enumerate the output tuples of
Q^{*}
via the enumeration algorithm for
signed
-acyclic
full

\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
queries presented in Section
4
; then we plug the tuples into
\varphi
to get its corresponding weight.

Now we present the construction of
Q^{*}
and
\mathcal{D}^{*}
. First, the desired
Q^{*}
is the following full
\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
query associated with
\mathcal{H}
:

(7)

\displaystyle Q^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}}R^{*}_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\wedge\bigwedge_{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}}\neg R^{*}_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N}),

where for every
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
, we add a positive atom
R^{*}_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})
, and for every
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
, we add a negative atom
R^{*}_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})
(and place a
\neg
symbol in front) into the body of
Q^{*}
. It is easy to see that (
7
) is a full
signed
-acyclic
full

\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
query associated with
\mathcal{H}
. Next, we construct the corresponding database instance
\mathcal{D}^{*}
for every atom in
Q^{*}
defined as follows:

(8)

\displaystyle R^{*}_{K}

\displaystyle=\{\mathbf{a}_{K}\in R_{K}\mid R_{K}(\mathbf{a}_{K})\neq\mathbf{0}\},

\displaystyle\text{ for every }K\in\mathcal{E}^{+},

\displaystyle R^{*}_{N}

\displaystyle=\{\mathbf{a}_{N}\in R_{N}\mid R_{N}(\mathbf{a}_{N})=\mathbf{0}\},

\displaystyle\text{ for every }N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}.

Thus,
\mathcal{D}^{*}
is of size
O(|\mathcal{D}|)
and can be constructed in
O(|\mathcal{D}|)
time by scanning over the list representation of every factor in
\mathcal{D}
. We reason about the such a construction of
\mathsf{Enum}(Q^{*},\mathcal{D}^{*})
through the following theorem:

Theorem C.3.

Let
\varphi
be a
signed
-acyclic full
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query associated with a signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
over a commutative semiring
\mathbf{S}
, and
\mathcal{D}
be a corresponding database instance. Then, a tuple
\mathbf{a}_{[n]}
is an answer of
\varphi
(i.e.
\varphi(\mathbf{a}_{[n]})\neq\mathbf{0}
) if and only if
\mathbf{a}_{[n]}
is an answer of
Q^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{*})
(i.e.
\mathbf{a}_{[n]}\in Q^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{*})
, or
Q^{*}(\mathbf{a}_{[n]})=\mathsf{true}
), where
Q^{*}
and
\mathcal{D}^{*}
are defined in (
7
) and (
8
), respectively.

Proof.

We first prove the “if” direction. Suppose
\mathbf{a}=\mathbf{a}_{[n]}\in Q^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{*})
. Then, we have
\Pi_{K}\mathbf{a}\in R^{*}_{K}
, for every
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
, and
\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a}\notin R^{*}_{N}
, for every
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
. This implies that
R_{K}(\Pi_{K}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
, for every
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
. However, for every
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
, either
\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a}\notin R_{N}
, or
\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a}\in R_{N}\wedge R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. We prove that
\mathbf{a}\in\varphi(\mathcal{D})
, or in other words,
\psi(\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
, by induction on the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
following the rules of the
CFG
(
5
) in a bottom-up fashion. The base case
S=\emptyset
is trivial, because
\psi(\Pi_{\emptyset}\mathbf{a})=e
, where
e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
. We argue the inductive step. If
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
uses the production
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})::=\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
next, and by induction hypothesis
\psi(\Pi_{S^{-}}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
, then
\psi(\Pi_{S}\mathbf{a})=R_{K}(\Pi_{K}\mathbf{a})\otimes\psi(\Pi_{S^{-}}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. Otherwise,
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
uses the production
(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
as the next yield step, and by induction hypothesis
\psi(\Pi_{S^{-}}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
and
\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. We distinguish the following two cases:

•

If
\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a}\notin R_{N}
, then

\displaystyle R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\vdash\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\oplus\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N}}}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\otimes\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=\mathbf{0}\oplus\mathbf{1}\otimes\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}

•

If
\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a}\in R_{N}
and
R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
, then

\displaystyle R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\vdash\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\oplus\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N}}}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\otimes\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\oplus\mathbf{0}\otimes\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}

In both cases, we have
\psi(\Pi_{S}\mathbf{a})=(R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\vdash\psi(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\psi(\Pi_{S^{-}}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. Therefore,
\varphi(\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
.

We then prove the “only if” direction. Suppose
\varphi(\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. We prove that
\mathbf{a}\in Q^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{*})
, or in other words,
\Pi_{K}\mathbf{a}\in R^{*}_{K}
, for every
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}
, and
\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a}\notin R^{*}_{N}
, for every
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
. We prove by induction on the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
following the rules of the
CFG
(
5
) in a top-down fashion, i.e. tracing the derivation from the start symbol
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
of the
CFG
(
5
). The base case is simply
\varphi(\mathbf{a})=\psi(\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. We now argue the inductive step and suppose
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
(
S\neq\emptyset
) is an intermediate non-terminal along the derivation of
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
. By inductive hypothesis, we have that
\psi(\Pi_{S}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. We then distinguish the following two cases:

•

If
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
uses the production
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})::=R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
next, then
\psi(\mathbf{a}_{S^{-}})\neq\mathbf{0}
and
R_{K}(\Pi_{K}\mathbf{a})\neq\mathbf{0}
. Therefore,
\Pi_{K}\mathbf{a}\in R^{*}_{K}
.

•

If
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
uses the production
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})::=(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
next, then
\psi(\mathbf{a}_{S^{-}})\neq\mathbf{0}
and
(R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\vdash\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a}))\neq\mathbf{0}
. The latter further implies that
\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a}\notin R^{*}_{N}
because if not, then

\displaystyle R_{N}(\Pi_{N}\mathbf{a})\vdash\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=\mathbf{0}\oplus\mathbf{0}\otimes\psi(\Pi_{N^{-}}\mathbf{a})=\mathbf{0}

and we get a contradiction. Thus, we have proven that
\mathbf{a}\in Q^{*}(\mathcal{D}^{*})
and close the proof.

∎

An immediate corrollary from
Theorem C.3
is the following, as a special case of
Theorem C.2
.

Corollary C.4.

Let
\varphi
be a
full

signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query over a commutative semiring
\mathbf{S}
. Then, there is an algorithm that can enumerate the answers of
\varphi
in
O(|\varphi|)
delay, after a
O(|\varphi|^{3}+|\varphi|\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
preprocessing time.

From the next section onwards, we study the enumeration problem of free-connex
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
queries.

C.2.
Enumeration of free-connex
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}

In the following sections, we let
\varphi
be a free-connex
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query (
6
) with free variables
F=[f]
and
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
be its associated signed hypergraph. We let
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
be the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression of
\varphi
, recognized by the
CFG
defined in (
5
). As
\mathcal{H}
is
signed
-acyclic, W.L.O.G, it accepts a
signed
-elimination sequence
(1,2,\dots,f\dots,n)
.

On a high level, our preprocessing algorithm takes as input
(i)
the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
, and
(ii)
its database instance
\mathcal{D}
, and for each
i=n-1,n-2,\dots,f
(following the
signed
-elimination order), it executes a
signed
-elimination step on
i
, that constructs an intermediate
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{[i]})
and its database instance
\mathcal{D}_{i}
, where

•

\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{[i]})
is associated with the signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{i}=\langle{\mathcal{H}_{i+1}},{i+1}\rangle
, i.e. the signed hypergraph after eliminating
n,n-1,\dots,i+1
from
\mathcal{H}
, (if
i=n-1
, then
\mathcal{H}_{n-1}=\langle{\mathcal{H}},{n}\rangle
)

•

\mathcal{D}_{i}
is an intermediate database instance of
\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{[i]})
such that
\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{[i]})=\bigoplus_{x_{i+1}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{i+1})}\psi_{i+1}(\mathbf{x}_{[i+1]})
. In other words, for any
\mathbf{a}_{[i]}\in\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{[i]})
, we have
\psi_{i}(\mathbf{a}_{[i]})=\bigoplus_{x_{i+1}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{i+1})}\psi(x_{i+1},\mathbf{a}_{[i]})
, where
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[i+1]})
is evaluated on
\mathcal{D}_{{i+1}}
and
\psi_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{[i]})
is evaluated on
\mathcal{D}_{i}
.

The last
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi_{f}(\mathbf{x}_{[f]})
, after the sequence of
signed
-elimination steps on
n-1,n-2,\dots,f
, becomes a full
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query whose associated signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{f}
accepts a
signed
-elimination sequence
(1,2,\dots,f)
and

\displaystyle\varphi(\mathbf{x}_{F})=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{x}_{[n]\setminus F}\in\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]\setminus F})}\;\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]\setminus F}\in\mathsf{Dom}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]\setminus F})}\;\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})=\cdots=\psi_{f}(\mathbf{x}_{[f]}).

Thus, we degenerate to the case where
\varphi
is a full
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query and our algorithm simply follows our discussion in the last section (Appendix
C.1
): reduce
\mathsf{Enum}(\psi_{f}(\mathbf{x}_{[f]}),\mathcal{D}_{f})
into
\mathsf{Enum}(Q^{*}_{f}(\mathbf{x}_{[f]}),\mathcal{D}^{*}_{f})
, where
Q^{*}_{f}
is a full
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
and apply the preprocessing algorithm for full
\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
. At the enumeration phase, we emit each answer
\mathbf{a}_{F}
of
Q^{*}_{f}(\mathcal{D}_{f})
(applying the enumeration algorithm for full
\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
) and plug the emitted tuple
\mathbf{a}_{F}
into
\psi_{f}
to get recover its weight
\psi_{f}(\mathbf{a}_{F})=\varphi(\mathbf{a}_{F})
.

signed
-elimination step.

W.L.O.G, we let
n
be the given
signed
-leaf of
\mathcal{H}
and appoint
U
to be a pivot hyperedge, breaking ties arbitrarily. We call the factor
R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
corresponding to
U
the
pivot factor
. A
signed
-elimination step runs the following 3 algorithms consecutively:

(1)

the refactoring algorithm (Appendix
C.3
)
The refactoring algorithm takes the given
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
and database instance
\mathcal{D}
as input and returns
(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]}),\mathcal{D}_{n})
as output where

•

\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
is a new
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression recognized by the following (more restrictive)
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
associated with the signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{n}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+}_{n},\mathcal{E}^{-}_{n})
, where
\mathcal{E}^{+}_{n}=\{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}\mid n\notin K\}\cup\{U\}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{+}
and
\mathcal{E}^{-}_{n}=\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\notin N\vee n\in U\subset N\}\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{-}
,

(9)

\displaystyle\framebox{$\textsf{CFG}_{n}$}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }S\subseteq[n],

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle::=\;e

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=\emptyset\text{ and }e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle::=\;R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=U

\displaystyle\mid\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=K\cup S^{-}\text{ and }K\in\{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}\mid n\notin K\}

\displaystyle\mid\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\text{ and }N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\in U\subset N\}

\displaystyle\mid\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\cup S^{-}\text{ and }N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\notin N\}

•

\mathcal{D}_{n}
is the corresponding database instance of
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
such that
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
. We observe that
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
is more restrictive than
CFG
(
5
) in that it has strictly less productions for the non-terminals
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S})
where
n\in S
. The refactoring algorithm uses the pivot factor
R_{U}
to “absorb” the factors
R_{K}
and
R_{N}
where
n\in K\subseteq U
,
n\in N\subseteq U
. By the properties of a
signed
-leaf
n
, the only hyperedges containing
n
left in
\mathcal{H}_{n}
are
U
and
N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
for some
k
such that
U\subseteq N_{1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq N_{k}
.

(2)

the oracle-construction algorithm (Appendix
C.5
)
The oracle-construction algorithm takes the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
(
9
), and the database instance
\mathcal{D}_{n}
from the refactoring algorithm and constructs an oracle data structure for the following
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpressions show up in
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
:
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
and
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{i}})
, where
i\in[k]
. The oracle-construction algorithm is analogous to the
\beta
-step in the enumeration of full
\mathsf{CQ}^{\neg}
queries, but instead of linked lists, it builds arrays and
RangeSum
data structures that support fast aggregation in the upcoming aggregation algorithm.

(3)

the aggregation algorithm (Appendix
C.4
)
The aggregation algorithm takes
(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]}),\mathcal{D}_{n})
and uses the constructed oracles
to finally constructs the desired
(\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{n-1}),\mathcal{D}_{n-1})
, where

•

\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{n-1})
is a new
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression recognized by the grammar
\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}
associated with
\mathcal{H}_{n-1}=\langle{\mathcal{H}},{n}\rangle
, or equivalently,
\mathcal{H}_{n-1}=([n-1],\mathcal{E}^{+}_{n-1},\mathcal{E}^{-}_{n-1})
, where
\mathcal{E}^{+}_{n-1}=\{K\setminus\{n\}\mid K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{E}^{-}_{n-1}=\{N\setminus\{n\}\mid N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}_{n}\}
. The grammar
\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}
is defined as follows:

(10)

\displaystyle\framebox{$\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}$}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }S\subseteq[n-1],

\displaystyle\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle::=\;e

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=\emptyset\text{ and }e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}

\displaystyle\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle::=\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}),

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=K\cup S^{-}\text{ and }K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}_{n-1},S^{-}\subseteq S

\displaystyle\mid\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}),

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\cup S^{-},N^{-}\subseteq N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}_{n-1}\text{ and }S^{-}\subseteq S

•

\mathcal{D}_{n-1}
is the corresponding database instance of
\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{n-1})
such that
\psi_{n-1}(\mathcal{D}_{n-1})=\bigoplus_{x_{n}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{n})}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
.

As a recap, a
signed
-elimination step on
n
aggregates out
x_{n}
from the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
and outputs a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})
associated with
\mathcal{H}_{n-1}=\langle{\mathcal{H}},{n}\rangle
and its database instance
\mathcal{D}_{n-1}
such that
\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})=\bigoplus_{x_{n}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{n})}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
. Now, we can keep applying the
signed
-elimination step on the next
signed
-leaf
n-1
and so on, untill all variables in
[n]\setminus F
have been removed.

C.3.
The refactoring algorithm

In this section, we present the refactoring algorithm that refactors
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
into
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
, a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression associated with
\mathcal{H}_{n}
. We start from
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
, the derivation in
CFG
(
5
), and refactor it step-by-step into a derivation of
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
(
9
), i.e.
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
. Along the way, we will also update the database instance
\mathcal{D}
accordingly, leading to
\mathcal{D}_{n}
at the end. In particular, we let

\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1},\ell(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})\right),

where
\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})\right)
, for some
M,S\subseteq[n]
, is an invokation of a recursive algorithm
\mathsf{Refactor}
that takes the following inputs:

(1)

a stashed derivation of a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, i.e.
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, such that its first yield step will be maintained to be one of the followings:

•

if
n\notin M
, then it follows that
M=\emptyset
and
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1}
; or

•

otherwise
n\in M
, then it applies the production of
CFG
that uses the largest hyperedge among all productions that can be used as the next yield step, if there is any (an arbitrary choice otherwise)

(2)

a derivation of a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
, i.e.
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
, for some
S\subseteq[n]
;

(3)

a database instance
\mathcal{D}
of
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
as a global input,

and returns a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
and a database instance
\mathcal{D}_{n}
corresponding to
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
, such that
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
. The refactoring algorithm
\mathsf{Refactor}
is illustrated in Algorithm
8
and formally, we prove the following lemma.

Input:

two derivations in
CFG
,
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
and
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
, for some
M,S\subseteq[n]

Input:

a global database instance
\mathcal{D}
of
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})

Output:

an expression
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
such that
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
.

if

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1}

then

1

if

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1}

then

\triangleright
base case

2

return

\mathbf{1}
;

3

else if

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})

then

\triangleright
(2.2)

4

\widehat{R_{U}}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\leftarrow R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})
;

5

return

\widehat{R_{U}}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
;

6

else if

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})

then

\triangleright
(2.1)

7

\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\leftarrow R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})
;

8

return

\left(\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\right)
;

9

else if

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow e

then

\triangleright
(3.3)

10

return

e\otimes e_{\emptyset}\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\widetilde{\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mu}(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mathbf{1})
;

11

12

else if

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

then

\triangleright

K\in\mathcal{E}^{+},S^{-}\subseteq[n]
such that
S=K\cup S^{-}

13

if

n\notin K

then

\triangleright
(3.1)

14

return

R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))
;

15

else

\triangleright
(4.1)

16

augment
the stash derivation
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
into

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})\Rightarrow R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})
;

17

return

\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))
;

18

else if

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

then

\triangleright

N\in\mathcal{E}^{-},N^{-}\subseteq N,S^{-}\subseteq[n]
such that
S=N\cup S^{-}

19

if

n\notin N

then

\triangleright
(3.2)

20

return

(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))
;

21

else

\triangleright
(4.2)

22

augment
the stash derivation
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
into

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})\Rightarrow\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})
;

23

return

\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))
;

24

Algorithm 8

The refactoring algorithm
\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S}))

Lemma C.5.

Let
\mathcal{H}=([n],\mathcal{E}^{+},\mathcal{E}^{-})
be a signed hypergraph,
n
be a
signed
-leaf of
\mathcal{H}
and
U
be a pivot hyperedge. Let
\varphi
be a free-connex
signed
-acyclic
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
query (
6
) associated with
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{D}
be a (global) database instance of
\varphi
. There is an algorithm that takes the following as input

(1)

a derivation of a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, i.e.
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, for some
M\subseteq[n]
such that if
n\notin M
, then
M=\emptyset
and
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1}
;

(2)

a derivation of a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
, i.e.
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
, for some
S\subseteq[n]
;

(3)

a global database instance
\mathcal{D}
of
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})

and then returns a subexpression
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
of
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
(
9
) such that
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
and a corresponding (global) database instance
\mathcal{D}_{n}
such that

(1)

\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
,

(2)

|\mathcal{D}_{n}|=O(|\mathcal{D}|)
,

(3)

the length of the derivation
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
is at most
|\eta|
, where
|\eta|
is the length of the derivation
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
plus the length of the derivation
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
.

(4)

runs in time
O(|\eta|^{2}+d(n)\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
, where
d(n)
is the number of hyperedges in
\mathcal{H}
that contains
n
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma by induction on
|\eta|\geq 2
for every if-else branch in
\mathsf{Refactor}
. We first examine the case where
the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
is
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\Rightarrow\psi(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})=\mathbf{1}
, so
S=\emptyset
. We divide into
2
main cases (case (1) and (2)), one for each next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
:

(1)

(
algorithm 8
) the base case is when
M=\emptyset
and
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow\mu(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})=\mathbf{1}
, in which case (
M=S=\emptyset,|\mu|=|\psi|=1
) we simply return
\mathbf{1}
and the lemma is trivially true. Indeed, it uses the production
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})::=\mathbf{1}
in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
.

(2)

otherwise, (
n\in M)
, recall
S=\emptyset
now, so there must be one largest hyperedge in
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, by the
\beta
-property of the
signed
-leaf
n
and we have the following cases:

(2.1)

(
algorithm 8
) let the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
be

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})

where
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}
,
N^{-}\subseteq N
and
M^{-}\subseteq M
such that
M=N\cup M^{-}
. As
N
is the largest hyperedge used among the next yield steps of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, we have
M^{-}\subseteq N
. Then we have

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\;\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\left(\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\right)

where
\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})=R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})
is an updated negative factor that can be computed in
O(|R_{N}|)
time. Recall that we return in this case

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\right)}.

Now, we observe that the first yield step in the stashed derivation
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{M})
is trimmed off in this inner recursive call. That is,
|\eta|
decrements by 1 in the recursion. By the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}\cup N^{-}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}\cup N^{-}})=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})=\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right).

Thus, we get

\displaystyle\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle=(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\left(\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\right).

and
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
indeed derives this subexpression in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
because (now
S=\emptyset
and
M=N
, so
M\cup S=N
)

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})

\displaystyle\;\Rightarrow\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}\cup N^{-}})

\displaystyle\;\Rightarrow\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}\cup N^{-}})

\displaystyle\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}\cup N^{-}})

(induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle\;=\widehat{R_{N}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\left(\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)

\displaystyle\;=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})

where
the first yield step here uses the production
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N})::=R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})
, where
S=N
and
N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\in U\subset N\}
.

(2.2)

(
algorithm 8
) if there is no
N\supseteq U
(only
N\subset U
lower), then in the children of the root, there must be
R_{U}
, because
R_{U}
can no longer hide lower in any subtrees of the AST, thus let the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
be

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})

where
M^{-}\subseteq M
. As
U
is the largest hyperedge used among the next yield steps of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, we have
M^{-}\subseteq U
. Then we have

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})\otimes\mathbf{1}

\displaystyle=\;\widehat{R_{U}}(\mathbf{x}_{U})

where
\widehat{R_{U}}(\mathbf{x}_{U})=R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M^{-}})
is an updated positive factor that can be computed in
O(|R_{U}|)
time.Recall that we simply return in this case
\widehat{R_{U}}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
and the lemma is trivially true. Indeed, the next production of
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
to be used is
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{U})::=R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
.

Next, we examine the case where
the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
is not
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1}
. Now,
\mathsf{Refactor}
prioritizes processing the next yield steps of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
over those of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
. We distinguish the following two cases: one for
n
not being contained in the hyperedge used in the next yield step, and the other for the opposite.

(3)

First, we examine the case where
n
is not contained in the hyperedge in the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
. There are 3 subcases, one for each next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
:

(3.1)

(
algorithm 8
)
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
, for some
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+},S^{-}\subseteq[n]
such that
S=K\cup S^{-}
. If
n\notin K
, then by the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})=\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))

since
|\eta|
decrements by 1 (the first yield step
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
is trimmed off in the recursive call). In the
CFG
, we have

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\left(R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\left(\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\right)

\displaystyle=\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))

(recall that we return
R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))
). Moreover,
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
derives this expression in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
because

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\Rightarrow R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})

where
the first yield step here uses the production
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S})::=R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
, where
S=K\cup S^{-}
and
K\in\{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}\mid n\notin K\}
.

(3.2)

(
algorithm 8
)
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
, for some
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-},N^{-}\subseteq N
, and
S^{-}\subseteq[n]
such that
S=N\cup S^{-}
. If
n\notin N
, then by the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})=\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))

since
|\eta|
decrements by 1 (the first yield step
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
is trimmed off). Indeed, in the
CFG
, we have

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle=\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\left(\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\right)

\displaystyle=\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))

(recall that we return
(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}}))
). Moreover,
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
derives this expression in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
because

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}}).

where
(i) the first yield step here uses the production
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})::=(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S^{-}})
, where
S=N\cup S^{-}
and
N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\notin N\}
, (ii) the second step is valid because
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})
in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
as
n\notin N^{-}
and (iii) the last derivation follows from the induction hypothesis.

(3.3)

(
algorithm 8
) Lastly, if
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow e
(so
S=\emptyset
). In the
CFG
, let
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\Rightarrow\widetilde{\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})
, where
\widetilde{\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mu}(\mathbf{x}_{M})
and
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\Rightarrow e_{\emptyset}\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;\left(\widetilde{\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\right)\otimes e

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;\left(\widetilde{\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes e_{\emptyset}\right)\otimes e

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\;(e\otimes e_{\emptyset})\otimes\widetilde{\mu}(\mathbf{x}_{M})

\displaystyle\text{ where }\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})=\widetilde{\mu}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes e_{\emptyset}

\displaystyle=\;(e\otimes e_{\emptyset})\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\widetilde{\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mu}(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1})

Recall that we return in this case
e\otimes e_{\emptyset}\otimes\mathsf{Refactor}(\widetilde{\ell}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mu}(\mathbf{x}_{M}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1})
. The yield step
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow e
is trimmed off, so
|\eta|
decrements by 1 in the recursive call.
Moreover,
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})
derives this expression in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
because

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})=\widetilde{\ell_{n}}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\Rightarrow\widetilde{\ell_{n}}(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup S})\otimes(e\otimes e_{\emptyset})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mu_{n}}(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes(e\otimes e_{\emptyset})=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes e

where
the first yield step here uses the production
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})::=e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
.

(4)

otherwise,
n
is contained in the hyperedge in the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
. In this case, we apply a ‘stash’ step that temporarily ‘stashes’ this yield step into
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
and then keep processing the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
.

(4.1)

(
algorithm 8
)
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
, for some
K\in\mathcal{E}^{+},S^{-}\subseteq[n]
such that
S=K\cup S^{-}
. Now
n\in K
. Let
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})=R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, we have

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\;\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle=\;\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

We insert a new yield step for
R_{K}
into the stashed derivation
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
and keep track of the largest hyperedge to be used as the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
in the augmented derivation. W.L.O.G, the agumented derivation becomes

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})\Rightarrow R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})

and then we return
\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup K}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\right)
.

(4.2)

(
algorithm 8
)
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\Rightarrow(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})
, for some
N\in\mathcal{E}^{-},N^{-}\subseteq N
, and
S^{-}\subseteq[n]
such that
S=N\cup S^{-}
. Now
n\in N
. Let
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})=\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
, we have

\displaystyle\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\;\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\otimes\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle=\;\left[\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})\right]\otimes\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle=\;\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})\Rightarrow\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\otimes\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\mu(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)\otimes\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})=\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})

and then we return
\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\right)
.

Case (4) is the only if-else branch where
|\eta|
stays the same in the recursive call, because this step essentially ‘stashes’ the next yield step of
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})
into the derivation for
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})
. Therefore, the length of the derivation for
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
decrements by 1 but that of
\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
increments by 1 in the recursive call. However, in the worst-case, this if-else branch can be visited at most
O(|\eta|)
times, each visit incurs an
O(|\eta|)
time overhead to place the yield step of
R_{N}
into the stashed derivation
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})
so that the largest hyperedge can be directly accessed upon request. Eventually,
\mathsf{Refactor}
will recurse back to one of the previous cases and
|\eta|
will decrement by 1 in that subsequent recursive call. As a result, the correctness of the lemma in this case simply follows through from the correctness of
\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M\cup N}),\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})\right)
.

Lastly, we justify that
\mathsf{Refactor}
runs in time
O(|\eta|^{2}+d(n)\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
. The
\mathsf{Refactor}
algorithm recursively visits each yield step in both derivations
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mu(\mathbf{x}_{M})
and
\ell(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{S})
at most twice (accounting for “stashes”). For each visit, if it does not fall into the last stash case (4),
|\eta|
decrements by 1, and
\mathsf{Refactor}
takes
O(|\mathcal{D}|)
time to update the global database instance
\mathcal{D}
. Furthermore, every such update (on the database instance of
\mathcal{D}
) leverages the
\alpha
or
\beta
properties of the
signed
-leaf
n
to guarantee that
|\mathcal{D}_{n}|\leq|\mathcal{D}|
and the update can only happen once for each hyperedge containing
n
(since that corresponding factor does not appear in the subsequent recursive call). Thus, all updates leading to
\mathcal{D}_{n}
take
O(d(n)\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
time in total.

For the last case (4), as discussed in paragraph of case (4),
\mathsf{Refactor}
can fall into it for at most
O(|\eta|)
times, each time with a
O(|\eta|)
time overhead before the next recursive call (for the ‘stashes’). Therefore, the total time complexity of
\mathsf{Refactor}
is
O(|\eta|^{2}+d(n)\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
.
∎

The following theorem is a direct consequence of Lemma
C.5
.

Theorem C.6.

There is an algorithm that takes as input an
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
associated with
\mathcal{H}
, thus recognized by
CFG
(
5
), and a database instance
\mathcal{D}
, and then returns a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
associated with
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, thus recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
(
9
), and a new database instance
\mathcal{D}_{n}
such that
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
,
|\psi_{n}|\leq|\psi|
and
|\mathcal{D}_{n}|\leq|\mathcal{D}|
. Moreover, the algorithm runs in time
O(|\psi|^{2}+d(n)\cdot|\mathcal{D}|)
, where
d(n)
is the number of hyperedges containing
n
in
\mathcal{H}
.

Proof.

We simply call
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\mathsf{Refactor}\left(\ell(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})\Rightarrow\mathbf{1},\ell(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})\right)
and trace the recursive call steps of
\mathsf{Refactor}
to construct the derivation
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
using production rules of
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
(
9
). As
\mathsf{Refactor}
(Algorithm
8
) only recurses on
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S})
where
n\in S
, and if not, it just follows from the original derivations in
CFG
(those derivations can be attached to the end of the derivation for every
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S})
where
n\notin S
). We therefore close the proof by invoking Lemma
C.5
.
∎

C.4.
The aggregation algorithm

We assume in this section that we have obtained
(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]}),\mathcal{D}_{n})
from the call of
\mathsf{Refactor}
as in Theorem
C.6
. We now shift our focus directly to the aggregation algorithm that eliminates
x_{n}
, where
n
is the
signed
-leaf of the signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}
. We defer the oracle-construction algorithm to Appendix
C.5
for now because the aggregation algorithm will provide an intuitive motivation that necessitates the oracles to be constructed.

The aggregation algorithm takes as input
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
(
9
) and a database instance
\mathcal{D}_{n}
and returns a new
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})
recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}
(
10
), and a corresponding database instance
\mathcal{D}_{n-1}
such that
\bigoplus_{x_{n}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{n})}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})=\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})
. As a recap, the production rules of
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
are: for all
S\subseteq[n]
,

(11)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle::=\;e

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=\emptyset\text{ and }e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}

(12)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S})

\displaystyle::=\;R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=U

(13)

\displaystyle\;\;\mid\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=K\cup S^{-}\text{ and }K\in\{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}\mid n\notin K\}

(14)

\displaystyle\;\;\mid\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\text{ and }N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\in U\subset N\}

(15)

\displaystyle\;\;\mid\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\cup S^{-}\text{ and }N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\notin N\}

The main upside of turning the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
(recognized by
CFG
) into
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
(recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
) is that the productions of
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
are layed out in a principled way:
x_{n}
is only contained in one terminal (or non-terminal). From an
\mathsf{AST}
standpoint, after refactoring,
x_{n}
only resides in at most one subtree at every intermediate
\bigotimes
node.

We safely assume that
R_{N_{k}},R_{N_{k-1}},\cdots,R_{N_{1}},R_{U}
are the only factors of
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
that contain
x_{n}
in
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
and
N_{k}\supseteq N_{k-1}\supseteq\cdots\supseteq N_{1}\supseteq U
(we let
N_{0}=U
for convenience), gifted by the
\beta
-property of the
signed
-leaf
n
. A convenient way to derive
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
in
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
is as follows (let
N_{i}\supseteq N^{-}_{i}=S_{i-1}\cup N_{i-1}
for
i\in[k]
)

(16)

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{k}})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}})\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k-1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{k-1}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\vdots

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k-1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}})\vdash\cdots R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\left(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{1}})\otimes(R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{1}}))\right)\cdots\right)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k-1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}})\vdash\cdots R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\left(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{1}})\otimes(R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{0}})\otimes\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{U}))\right)\cdots\right)\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k-1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}})\vdash\cdots R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\left(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{1}})\otimes(R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{0}})\otimes R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U}))\right)\cdots\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\;\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})

where every
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}
(a derive step) in (
16
) applies the productions (
11
), (
13
) and (
15
) to factor out all the terms (
e,R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})
or
R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})
) that does not contain
x_{n}
and wrap them in some
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{i}})
such that
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{i}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle*}}{{\Rightarrow}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{i}})
, where
i\in[k]
and
n\notin S_{i}
. Then it is followed by
\Rightarrow
(a yield step) that applies the production (
14
) that produces the negative factors
R_{N_{k}},R_{N_{k-1}},\cdots,R_{N_{1}}
from
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}}),\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}}),\cdots,\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})
, except for the last yield step where we use the production (
12
) to produce the pivot factor
R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
from
\ell_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
.

On a high level, our aggregation algorithm will follow the derivation (
16
) of
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
line-by-line and “push-in” the aggregation operator
\bigoplus_{x_{n}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{n})}
into the more and more nested subexpressions. For succinctness, we use
\bigoplus_{x_{n}}
as an abbreviation of
\bigoplus_{x_{n}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{n})}
in the following. This “push-in” step for every derive step is immediate: we can simply push the aggregation operator
\bigoplus_{x_{n}}
by factoring out the term
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{i}})
that does not contain
x_{n}
. That is, for
i=0,1,\ldots,k
,

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{i}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{i}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{i}})\right)

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{i}})\otimes\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\left(R_{N_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{i}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{i}})\right)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{0}})\otimes R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{0}})\otimes\bigoplus_{x_{n}}R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{0}})\otimes R_{U\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{U\setminus\{n\}})

Here, in the last yield step (applying the production (
12
)),
\bigoplus_{x_{n}}R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U})
is straightforward: we can scan the list representation of the pivot factor
R_{U}
once and get a new factor
R_{U\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{U\setminus\{n\}})
in time
O(|R_{U}|)
.

Now we turn to the tricky yield steps in the derivation (
16
) that applies the production (
14
) of the
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
. What we want here is to “push-in” the aggregation operator
\bigoplus_{x_{n}}
after the
\vdash
operator so that the aggregation algorithm can proceed with the subexpression
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})
. To that end, first we recall the semantics of the
\vdash
operator:

\bigoplus_{x_{n}}R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})=\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\oplus\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N}}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\otimes\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right).

We distinguish the following two disjoint cases:

(1)

if
\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}}\notin\Pi_{N\setminus\{n\}}R_{N}
, then the aggreation simply becomes
\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})
, because
R_{N}(x_{n},\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}})=\mathbf{0}
and
\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N}}}(x_{n},\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}})=\mathbf{1}
, for all
x_{n}\in\mathsf{Dom}(x_{n})
. Thus, for such tuples
\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}}
, the push-in step is trivial.

(2)

otherwise, we have
\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}}\in\Pi_{N\setminus\{n\}}R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})
. In this case, we define a new factor
R_{N\setminus\{n\}}
as follows:

\displaystyle R_{N\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}})=\begin{cases}\bigoplus_{x_{n}}R_{N}({x_{n},\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}}})\oplus\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N}}}({x_{n},\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}}})\otimes\psi_{n}({x_{n},\mathbf{a}_{N^{-}\setminus\{n\}}})&\text{ if }\mathbf{a}_{N\setminus\{n\}}\in\Pi_{N\setminus\{n\}}R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\\
\mathbf{0}&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

It is easy to see that the list representation of
R_{N\setminus\{n\}}
is of size
O(|R_{N}|)
.

Therefore, if we can obtain (the list representation of) this new factor
R_{N\setminus\{n\}}
efficiently, the “push-in” of the aggregation operator becomes immediate because

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\left(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\oplus\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N}}}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\otimes\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})\right)

\displaystyle=R_{N\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{N\setminus\{n\}})\oplus\mathbbm{1}_{{\neg R_{N\setminus\{n\}}}}(\mathbf{x}_{N\setminus\{n\}})\otimes\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})

\displaystyle=R_{N\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{N\setminus\{n\}})\vdash\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}})

Back to our big picture, the aggregation step “pushes-in” the aggregation operator
\bigoplus_{x_{n}}
one line at a time following the derivation (
16
):

(17)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}\setminus\{n\}})\vdash\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{k}})\right)

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}\setminus\{n\}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}})\right)

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}\setminus\{n\}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k-1}\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k-1}\setminus\{n\}})\vdash\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{k-1}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(\cdots R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\left(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{1}})\otimes(R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}_{1}}))\right)\cdots\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(\cdots R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\left(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{1}})\otimes(R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{0}})\otimes\bigoplus_{x_{n}}\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{U}))\right)\cdots\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k}})\otimes\left(R_{N_{k}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{k}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{k-1}})\otimes\left(\cdots R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\left(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{1}})\otimes(R_{N_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{N_{1}})\vdash\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{S_{0}})\otimes\bigoplus_{x_{n}}R_{U}(\mathbf{x}_{U}))\right)\cdots\right)\right)

\displaystyle=:\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})

As one can see from the comparison with (
16
), the derivation of
\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})
exactly follows the derivation of
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
except that
x_{n}
has been peeled off from the set of variables and every
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
subexpression show up in
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
. So,
\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})
is a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression recognized by the following intermediate context-free grammar, obtained by directly removing
x_{n}
from every non-terminal of the grammar
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
: for all
S\subseteq[n-1]
,

\displaystyle\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle::=\;e

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=\emptyset\text{ and }e\in\boldsymbol{D}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}

\displaystyle\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{S\setminus\{n\}})

\displaystyle::=\;R_{U\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{U\setminus\{n\}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=U

\displaystyle\;\;\mid\;R_{K}(\mathbf{x}_{K})\otimes\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}\setminus\{n\}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=K\cup S^{-}\text{ and }K\in\{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}\mid n\notin K\}

\displaystyle\;\;\mid\;(R_{N\setminus\{n\}}(\mathbf{x}_{N\setminus\{n\}})\vdash\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}\setminus\{n\}}))

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\setminus\{n\}\text{ and }N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\in U\subset N\}

\displaystyle\;\;\mid\;(R_{N}(\mathbf{x}_{N})\vdash\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{N^{-}}))\otimes\ell_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{S^{-}\setminus\{n\}})

\displaystyle\text{ where }S=N\cup S^{-}\text{ and }N^{-}\subseteq N\in\{N\in\mathcal{E}^{-}\mid n\notin N\}

In spite of its discrepencies to
\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}
(
10
), every production of the above grammar can be derived from
\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}
. Indeed, recall that
\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}
is associated with the signed hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{n-1}=\langle{\mathcal{H}},{n}\rangle=([n-1],\mathcal{E}_{n-1}^{+},\mathcal{E}_{n-1}^{-})
. Then,
U\setminus\{n\}\in\mathcal{E}_{n-1}^{+}
,
\{K\in\mathcal{E}^{+}\mid n\notin K\}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{n-1}^{+}
and
\{N\setminus\{n\}\mid N\in\mathcal{E}^{-},n\notin N\vee n\in U\subset N\}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{n-1}^{-}
. Therefore, the
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression
\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})
is also recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n-1}
at the end of the elimination step for the
signed
-leaf
n
and the
signed
-elimination step for
n
now completes.

The only missing (and tricky) piece is how to compute the list representation of the new factor
R_{N\setminus\{n\}}
efficiently. In the next section, we will introduce an efficient algorithm for this task using
RangeSum
data structures and it then to the following main theorem. Its proof is deferred to Appendix
C.6
, after the introduction of
RangeSum
data structures.

Theorem C.7.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{n}
be a signed hypergraph with a
signed
-leaf
n
. Let
\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]})
be a
\mathsf{NestFAQ}^{\neg}
expression associated with
\mathcal{M}_{n}
(thus recognized by
\textsf{CFG}_{n}
(
9
)) and
\mathcal{D}_{n}
be its database instance. There is an (aggregation) algorithm that takes
(\psi_{n}(\mathbf{x}_{[n]}),\mathcal{D}_{n})
as input and outputs
(\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]}),\mathcal{D}_{n-1})
where

(1)

\psi_{n-1}(\mathbf{x}_{[n-1]})
